They seemed concerned about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you try to limit your food intake to control your weight?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. It's... well, if the scale goes up even a fraction, I feel this sharp panic. It just feels like I’m losing the discipline I’ve worked so hard to maintain. So keeping my food intake small is definitely a way to make sure my weight stays exactly where I need it to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. My clothes are definitely looser now, and... well, the numbers on the scale have just kept drifting down. Honestly, part of me sees that as a sign that I’m staying focused. It feels like proof that I'm not letting all the chaos at work spill over into my own body.\n\nDoctor: Do you often think about the possibility of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yes, constantly. Like I mentioned a moment ago, the idea of the scale going up even a tiny bit brings on this really sharp panic. It's always in the back of my mind. Gaining weight would mean I'm losing that discipline I've built up, so... yes, I think about it pretty much all the time.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat an amount of food that you consider excessive in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really do that. My focus is always on keeping my intake as small and controlled as possible, so... no, eating a large amount of food at once isn't something that happens. I'm too careful for that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten an amount of food that you considered excessive in a short period of time, even if it's not a regular occurrence?\n\nPatient: No, I really haven't. Like I just mentioned, I don't let myself lose control like that. Even as a rare occurrence or a one-off... it just doesn't happen. I keep everything very measured and calculated. The idea of eating a large volume of food all at once is just completely contrary to how I operate.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. Like I was just saying, my eating is very structured. Control is... well, it's the main focus for me. I always know exactly what I'm consuming and how much, so I've never had a moment where I felt out of control or like I couldn't stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit or use laxatives after eating?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do either of those things. Since I strictly limit what I eat in the first place, there isn't really a need to try and get rid of it afterward. So, no, no vomiting or laxatives.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you are currently underweight?\n\nPatient: I know other people have expressed concern about it, but... I honestly think they're overstating the situation. I mean, I'm still functioning perfectly well. I go to work, I'm still doing all my experiments in the lab, and keeping up with my usual schedule. So, no, I don't feel like my weight is at a level that's medically dangerous.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight related to a fear of losing control or is it more about the appearance or other concerns?\n\nPatient: It's definitely about control. I mean, my work in the lab requires such precision, and I guess I apply that same standard to myself. If the scale goes up even a fraction of a pound, I feel this sharp panic... like I'm losing the discipline I've worked so hard to maintain. It's not really about looking a certain way, it's just about knowing I can manage my own body.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It's... well, if the scale goes up even a fraction, I feel this sharp panic. It just feels like I’m losing the discipline I’ve worked so hard to maintain. So keeping my food intake small is definitely a way to make sure my weight stays exactly where I need it to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. My clothes are definitely looser now, and... well, the numbers on the scale have just kept drifting down. Honestly, part of me sees that as a sign that I’m staying focused. Like I mentioned a moment ago, the idea of the scale going up even a tiny bit brings on this really sharp panic. It's always in the back of my mind. Gaining weight would mean I'm losing that discipline I've built up, so... yes, I think about it pretty much all the time.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat an amount of food that you consider excessive in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really do that. My focus is always on keeping my intake as small and controlled as possible, so... no, eating a large amount of food at once isn't something that happens. I'm too careful for that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten an amount of food that you considered excessive in a short period of time, even if it's not a regular occurrence?\n\nPatient: No, I really haven't. Like I just mentioned, I don't let myself lose control like that. Even as a rare occurrence or a one-off... it just doesn't happen. I keep everything very measured and calculated. The idea of eating a large volume of food all at once is just completely contrary to how I operate.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. Like I was just saying, my eating is very structured. Control is... well, it's the main focus for me. I always know exactly what I'm consuming and how much, so I've never had a moment where I felt out of control or like I couldn't stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit or use laxatives after eating?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do either of those things. Since I strictly limit what I eat in the first place, there isn't really a need to try and get rid of it afterward. So, no, no vomiting or laxatives.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you are currently underweight?\n\nPatient: I know other people have expressed concern about it, but... I honestly think they're overstating the situation. I mean, I'm still functioning perfectly well. I go to work, I'm still doing all my experiments in the lab, and keeping up with my usual schedule. So, no, I don't feel like my weight is at a level that's medically dangerous.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight related to a fear of losing control or is it more about the appearance or other concerns?\n\nPatient: It's definitely about control. It's not really about looking a certain way, it's just about knowing I can manage my own body.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any changes in your menstrual cycle?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed any changes. So, no issues there.\n\nDoctor: How much weight have you lost over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure of the exact number. It feels like proof that I'm not letting all the chaos at work spill over into my own body.\n\nDoctor: Do you often think about the possibility of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yes, constantly. Like I mentioned a moment ago, the idea of the scale going up even a tiny bit brings on this really sharp panic. It's always in the back of my mind. Gaining weight would mean I'm losing that discipline I've built up, so... yes, I think about it pretty much all the time.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat an amount of food that you consider excessive in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really do that. My focus is always on keeping my intake as small and controlled as possible, so... no, eating a large amount of food at once isn't something that happens. I'm too careful for that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten an amount of food that you considered excessive in a short period of time, even if it's not a regular occurrence?\n\nPatient: No, I really haven't. Like I just mentioned, I don't let myself lose control like that. Even as a rare occurrence or a one-off... it just doesn't happen. I keep everything very measured and calculated. The idea of eating a large volume of food all at once is just completely contrary to how I operate.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. Like I was just saying, my eating is very structured. Control is... well, it's the main focus for me. I always know exactly what I'm consuming and how much, so I've never had a moment where I felt out of control or like I couldn't stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit or use laxatives after eating?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do either of those things. Since I strictly limit what I eat in the first place, there isn't really a need to try and get rid of it afterward. So, no, no vomiting or laxatives.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you are currently underweight?\n\nPatient: I know other people have expressed concern about it, but... I honestly think they're overstating the situation. I mean, I'm still functioning perfectly well. I go to work, I'm still doing all my experiments in the lab, and keeping up with my usual schedule. So, no, I don't feel like my weight is at a level that's medically dangerous.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight related to a fear of losing control or is it more about the appearance or other concerns?\n\nPatient: It's definitely about control. I mean, my work in the lab requires such precision, and I guess I apply that same standard to myself. If the scale goes up even a fraction of a pound, I feel this sharp panic... like I'm losing the discipline I've worked so hard to maintain. It's not really about looking a certain way, it's just about knowing I can manage my own body.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any changes in your menstrual cycle?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed any changes. My cycle has always been fairly regular, and... well, it still is. So, no issues there.\n\nDoctor: How much weight have you lost over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure of the exact number. I mean, I weigh myself regularly to make sure it isn't going up, but I haven't really kept a running total of what I've lost overall over the past few months. So, I don't really know exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any menstrual periods in the past three months?\n\nPatient: Yes, like I said just a moment ago, my periods have been completely normal. I've had them regularly over the past three months.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."

So keeping my food intake small is definitely a way to make sure my weight stays exactly where I need it to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. My clothes are definitely looser now, and... well, the numbers on the scale have just kept drifting down. Honestly, part of me sees that as a sign that I’m staying focused. It feels like proof that I'm not letting all the chaos at work spill over into my own body.\n\nDoctor: Do you often think about the possibility of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yes, constantly. Like I mentioned a moment ago, the idea of the scale going up even a tiny bit brings on this really sharp panic. It's always in the back of my mind. I'm a biological scientist, and during long lab runs or when I'm prepping for an exhibition, I just push breakfast and lunch aside. And then, by the time I actually do sit down to eat, I just keep it to something very small and controlled. They seemed concerned about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you try to limit your food intake to control your weight?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. It's... well, if the scale goes up even a fraction, I feel this sharp panic. It just feels like I’m losing the discipline I’ve worked so hard to maintain. So keeping my food intake small is definitely a way to make sure my weight stays exactly where I need it to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. My clothes are definitely looser now, and... well, the numbers on the scale have just kept drifting down. Honestly, part of me sees that as a sign that I’m staying focused. It feels like proof that I'm not letting all the chaos at work spill over into my own body.\n\nDoctor: Do you often think about the possibility of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yes, constantly. Like I mentioned a moment ago, the idea of the scale going up even a tiny bit brings on this really sharp panic. It's always in the back of my mind. Gaining weight would mean I'm losing that discipline I've built up, so... yes, I think about it pretty much all the time.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat an amount of food that you consider excessive in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really do that. My focus is always on keeping my intake as small and controlled as possible, so... no, eating a large amount of food at once isn't something that happens. I'm too careful for that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten an amount of food that you considered excessive in a short period of time, even if it's not a regular occurrence?\n\nPatient: No, I really haven't. Like I just mentioned, I don't let myself lose control like that. Even as a rare occurrence or a one-off... it just doesn't happen. I keep everything very measured and calculated. The idea of eating a large volume of food all at once is just completely contrary to how I operate.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. Like I was just saying, my eating is very structured. Control is... well, it's the main focus for me. I always know exactly what I'm consuming and how much, so I've never had a moment where I felt out of control or like I couldn't stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit or use laxatives after eating?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do either of those things. Since I strictly limit what I eat in the first place, there isn't really a need to try and get rid of it afterward. So, no, no vomiting or laxatives.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you are currently underweight?\n\nPatient: I know other people have expressed concern about it, but... I honestly think they're overstating the situation. I mean, I'm still functioning perfectly well. I go to work, I'm still doing all my experiments in the lab, and keeping up with my usual schedule. So, no, I don't feel like my weight is at a level that's medically dangerous.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight related to a fear of losing control or is it more about the appearance or other concerns?\n\nPatient: It's definitely about control. I mean, my work in the lab requires such precision, and I guess I apply that same standard to myself. If the scale goes up even a fraction of a pound, I feel this sharp panic... like I'm losing the discipline I've worked so hard to maintain. It's not really about looking a certain way, it's just about knowing I can manage my own body.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any changes in your menstrual cycle?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed any changes. I just haven't run those numbers.\n\nDoctor: Has your weight loss been so significant that others have noticed and commented on it?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. So, no, no vomiting or laxatives.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you are currently underweight?\n\nPatient: I know other people have expressed concern about it, but... I honestly think they're overstating the situation. I mean, I'm still functioning perfectly well. I go to work, I'm still doing all my experiments in the lab, and keeping up with my usual schedule. So, no, I don't feel like my weight is at a level that's medically dangerous.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight related to a fear of losing control or is it more about the appearance or other concerns?\n\nPatient: It's definitely about control. I mean, my work in the lab requires such precision, and I guess I apply that same standard to myself. If the scale goes up even a fraction of a pound, I feel this sharp panic... like I'm losing the discipline I've worked so hard to maintain. It's not really about looking a certain way, it's just about knowing I can manage my own body.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any changes in your menstrual cycle?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed any changes. My cycle has always been fairly regular, and... well, it still is. So, no issues there.\n\nDoctor: How much weight have you lost over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure of the exact number. I mean, I weigh myself regularly to make sure it isn't going up, but I haven't really kept a running total of what I've lost overall over the past few months. So, I don't really know exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any menstrual periods in the past three months?\n\nPatient: Yes, like I said just a moment ago, my periods have been completely normal. I've had them regularly over the past three months.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your current weight is more than 15% below the normal weight range for your height?\n\nPatient: I haven't calculated the exact percentage, to be honest. But... my clothes are definitely looser, and the numbers on the scale have just kept drifting down. \n\nI don't really see it as a bad thing, though. Part of me sees that as a sign that I’m focused. It's like... it proves I'm not letting the chaos at work spill over into my body. So I'm just keeping things under control.\n\nDoctor: Do you currently weigh less than 85% of the normal weight range for someone of your height?\n\nPatient: I think that's essentially what you just asked me. Like I said, I haven't calculated the exact percentages. I know my weight is down, but I really don't know if it's below 85 percent of whatever is considered the normal range. I just haven't run those numbers.\n\nDoctor: Has your weight loss been so significant that others have noticed and commented on it?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, my colleagues at the lab are usually pretty focused on their own work. If anyone has noticed, they haven't actually said anything to me about it. Plus, I usually just wear a lab coat over my clothes during the day anyway, so I don't think it's something that really stands out to anyone else.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any changes in the heaviness or lightness of your menstrual flow over the past few months?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed any changes like that. My periods have just stopped entirely. I haven't had one in... a few months now, actually. Gaining weight would mean I'm losing that discipline I've built up, so... yes, I think about it pretty much all the time.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat an amount of food that you consider excessive in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really do that. My focus is always on keeping my intake as small and controlled as possible, so... no, eating a large amount of food at once isn't something that happens. I'm too careful for that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten an amount of food that you considered excessive in a short period of time, even if it's not a regular occurrence?\n\nPatient: No, I really haven't. Like I just mentioned, I don't let myself lose control like that. Even as a rare occurrence or a one-off... it just doesn't happen. I keep everything very measured and calculated. The idea of eating a large volume of food all at once is just completely contrary to how I operate.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. Like I was just saying, my eating is very structured. Control is... well, it's the main focus for me. I always know exactly what I'm consuming and how much, so I've never had a moment where I felt out of control or like I couldn't stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit or use laxatives after eating?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do either of those things. Since I strictly limit what I eat in the first place, there isn't really a need to try and get rid of it afterward. So, no, no vomiting or laxatives.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you are currently underweight?\n\nPatient: I know other people have expressed concern about it, but... I honestly think they're overstating the situation. I mean, I'm still functioning perfectly well. I go to work, I'm still doing all my experiments in the lab, and keeping up with my usual schedule. So, no, I don't feel like my weight is at a level that's medically dangerous.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight related to a fear of losing control or is it more about the appearance or other concerns?\n\nPatient: It's definitely about control. I mean, my work in the lab requires such precision, and I guess I apply that same standard to myself. If the scale goes up even a fraction of a pound, I feel this sharp panic... like I'm losing the discipline I've worked so hard to maintain. It's not really about looking a certain way, it's just about knowing I can manage my own body.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any changes in your menstrual cycle?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed any changes. My cycle has always been fairly regular, and... well, it still is. So, no issues there.\n\nDoctor: How much weight have you lost over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure of the exact number. I mean, I weigh myself regularly to make sure it isn't going up, but I haven't really kept a running total of what I've lost overall over the past few months. So, I don't really know exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any menstrual periods in the past three months?\n\nPatient: Yes, like I said just a moment ago, my periods have been completely normal. I've had them regularly over the past three months.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your current weight is more than 15% below the normal weight range for your height?\n\nPatient: I haven't calculated the exact percentage, to be honest. But... my clothes are definitely looser, and the numbers on the scale have just kept drifting down. \n\nI don't really see it as a bad thing, though. Part of me sees that as a sign that I’m focused. It's like... it proves I'm not letting the chaos at work spill over into my body. So I'm just keeping things under control.\n\nDoctor: Do you currently weigh less than 85% of the normal weight range for someone of your height?\n\nPatient: I think that's essentially what you just asked me. Like I said, I haven't calculated the exact percentages. I know my weight is down, but I really don't know if it's below 85 percent of whatever is considered the normal range. I just haven't run those numbers.\n\nDoctor: Has your weight loss been so significant that others have noticed and commented on it?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, my colleagues at the lab are usually pretty focused on their own work. If anyone has noticed, they haven't actually said anything to me about it. Plus, I usually just wear a lab coat over my clothes during the day anyway, so I don't think it's something that really stands out to anyone else.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any changes in the heaviness or lightness of your menstrual flow over the past few months?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed any changes like that. My periods have just stopped entirely. I haven't had one in... a few months now, actually. So there isn't really a flow to be heavy or light anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you've eaten an amount of food that's larger than what most people would eat in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. I actually eat very little. I've never had a time where I felt like I was eating a large amount of food like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.   },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."
